Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because my sleep has gotten really messed up. I work overnight shifts, and even when I get off and I’m exhausted, I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then if I do finally fall asleep, I wake up scared, and it feels like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up suddenly during the night feeling extremely scared, confused, or with a racing heart, even if you don’t remember a nightmare?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that does happen. I’ll finally doze off after my shift, and then I’ll wake up scared, like something’s wrong, even if I don’t really remember a dream. My sleep just feels broken up, and I wake up feeling like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do these sudden awakenings with fear or confusion happen specifically when you're trying to sleep at night, or can they happen at any time when you're resting?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m trying to sleep after my overnight shift. I’ll be really tired but still can’t fall asleep, and then if I do drift off, I wake up scared and feel like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: When you're awake, do you often feel unusually worried, tense, or on edge, even when there's no specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I feel worried all the time for no reason… I mostly just notice it around trying to sleep or when I wake up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you're awake and not trying to sleep, do you ever feel anxious or worried for no clear reason, or do your worries usually have a specific cause?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed feeling anxious out of nowhere when I’m just awake. If I’m worried, it’s usually about something specific, not just for no reason.\n\nDoctor: When you're fully awake, have you ever had sudden episodes of intense fear or discomfort that came out of nowhere and included physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t really noticed that happening when I’m fully awake. It’s more around when I’m trying to sleep or waking up, not just out of nowhere during the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever woken up and been unable to move or speak for a short period of time, either right after waking up or sometimes while falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been unable to move or talk like that. It’s more that my sleep is really broken up now. I get off my overnight shift and I’m still lying there wide awake, and then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared and feel like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: When you're awake and not trying to sleep, have you ever had upsetting thoughts, images, or worries that kept coming back and caused you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going back over things in my head, like every little thing I might’ve done wrong with my patients or with my family. It just keeps coming back, and I start feeling like I’m a worthless aide… like I didn’t protect people the way I should have. Sometimes it feels like God is mad at me for that.\n\nDoctor: Do these upsetting thoughts often feel like they're connected to something traumatic or very disturbing that you experienced before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think it’s tied to one specific traumatic thing. It’s more like worries and guilt that keep coming back, about things I feel I did wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do these upsetting thoughts feel like they’re not really you, or do they feel more like something you believe is true about yourself?\n\nPatient: They feel true… that’s the hard part. It doesn’t feel like some random thought that isn’t me. I keep thinking about every little thing I did wrong with patients or my family, and it feels like I really am a worthless aide. Like I didn’t protect people the way I should have, and God is mad at me for it.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about feeling worthless and not protecting people enough happen most days, and have they been going on for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s most days. It’s been going on for more than two weeks. I keep going over every little thing I think I did wrong with patients or with my family, and it just feels true that I’m a worthless aide… like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been thinking about it like that, like whether I enjoy things or not. Then if I do finally fall asleep, I wake up scared, and it feels like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up suddenly during the night feeling extremely scared, confused, or with a racing heart, even if you don’t remember a nightmare?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that does happen. I’ll finally doze off after my shift, and then I’ll wake up scared, like something’s wrong, even if I don’t really remember a dream. My sleep just feels broken up, and I wake up feeling like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do these sudden awakenings with fear or confusion happen specifically when you're trying to sleep at night, or can they happen at any time when you're resting?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m trying to sleep after my overnight shift. I’ll be really tired but still can’t fall asleep, and then if I do drift off, I wake up scared and feel like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: When you're awake, do you often feel unusually worried, tense, or on edge, even when there's no specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I feel worried all the time for no reason… I mostly just notice it around trying to sleep or when I wake up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you're awake and not trying to sleep, do you ever feel anxious or worried for no clear reason, or do your worries usually have a specific cause?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed feeling anxious out of nowhere when I’m just awake. If I’m worried, it’s usually about something specific, not just for no reason.\n\nDoctor: When you're fully awake, have you ever had sudden episodes of intense fear or discomfort that came out of nowhere and included physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t really noticed that happening when I’m fully awake. It’s more around when I’m trying to sleep or waking up, not just out of nowhere during the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever woken up and been unable to move or speak for a short period of time, either right after waking up or sometimes while falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been unable to move or talk like that. It’s more that my sleep is really broken up now. I get off my overnight shift and I’m still lying there wide awake, and then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared and feel like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: When you're awake and not trying to sleep, have you ever had upsetting thoughts, images, or worries that kept coming back and caused you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going back over things in my head, like every little thing I might’ve done wrong with my patients or with my family. It just keeps coming back, and I start feeling like I’m a worthless aide… like I didn’t protect people the way I should have. Sometimes it feels like God is mad at me for that.\n\nDoctor: Do these upsetting thoughts often feel like they're connected to something traumatic or very disturbing that you experienced before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think it’s tied to one specific traumatic thing. It’s more like worries and guilt that keep coming back, about things I feel I did wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do these upsetting thoughts feel like they’re not really you, or do they feel more like something you believe is true about yourself?\n\nPatient: They feel true… that’s the hard part. It doesn’t feel like some random thought that isn’t me. I keep thinking about every little thing I did wrong with patients or my family, and it feels like I really am a worthless aide. Like I didn’t protect people the way I should have, and God is mad at me for it.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about feeling worthless and not protecting people enough happen most days, and have they been going on for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s most days. It’s been going on for more than two weeks. I keep going over every little thing I think I did wrong with patients or with my family, and it just feels true that I’m a worthless aide… like God is mad at me because I didn’t protect people better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t paid much attention to that. I know I don’t feel like myself, but I can’t say for sure about enjoying things less.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in activities or things you used to enjoy, on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been paying attention to whether I enjoy things less. I just know I don’t feel right, but I can’t say for sure about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, even things you used to really like doing?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure. I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I enjoy things or not. I just feel off, but I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, even things you used to really like doing?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know. I haven’t been thinking about it like that, like whether I enjoy things or not. I just know I don’t feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in that exact way, like less pleasure. I just haven’t been thinking about things I used to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been keeping track of that, like if it’s most days or not. I just… I’m not sure how to tell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
